# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title employee Model

class Company {
    name : String
    departments : List<Department>
    employees : List<Employee>
    calculateTotalEmployeeSalary() : double
    calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission() : double
    calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums() : double
}
class Department {
    type : DepartmentType
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    manager : Manager
    calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours() : double
}
abstract class Employee {
    birthDate : EDate
    department : String
    name : String
    socialInsuranceNumber : String
    company : Company
}
class Manager {
    position : String
    salary : double
    subordinates : List<Employee>
    getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount() : int
}
class OffShiftWorker {
    officialHolidayPermit : boolean
    weekendPermit : boolean
}
class SalesPeople {
    amountOfSales : double
    commissionPercentage : double
    salary : double
    getTotalCommission() : double
}
class ShiftWorker {
    holidayPremium : double
    calculateHolidayPremium() : double
    setdepartment(department : String) : void
}
abstract class Worker {
    hourlyRates : double
    weeklyWorkingHour : int
}
enum DepartmentType {
    PRODUCTION
    CONTROL
    DELIVERY
}

Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Company *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department --> "*" Employee : employees
Department --> Manager : manager
Employee --> Company : company  'opposite: employees
Manager --|> Employee
Manager --> "*" Employee : subordinates
OffShiftWorker --|> Worker
SalesPeople --|> Employee
ShiftWorker --|> Worker
Worker --|> Employee
@enduml
```

Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize totalPremium to 0.
2. Traverse the company's employees list.
3. For each employee:
   - If the employee is a ShiftWorker, compute premium = holidayPremium.
   - Add the premium to totalPremium.
4. Round totalPremium to 2 decimal places.
5. Return totalPremium.

- name : String
Related Classes: Employee, ShiftWorker

- None.

- double value representing the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers.
- Monetary value rounded to two decimal places.

- The company instance exists.
- The employees list is initialized; it may be empty.
- All ShiftWorker employees must satisfy their department constraint, i.e. their department is "Delivery".

- No persistent state is modified.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.

4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

```plantuml

    weekendPermit : boolean
}

    DELIVERY
}

Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Operation Specifications
Company::calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums
Summary:
Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize totalPremium to 0.
2. For each employee in the company's employees list:
   2.1 If the employee is a ShiftWorker instance, compute its holiday premium.
   2.2 Add the computed premium to totalPremium.
3. Round totalPremium to two decimal places.
4. Return totalPremium.
Related Features:
- employees : List<Employee>
- calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums() : double
- holidayPremium : double
- calculateHolidayPremium() : double
- setdepartment(department : String) : void
Related Classes: Employee, ShiftWorker
Input:
- None.
- Operates on the current Company instance.
Output:
- double value representing the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company.
- The value is rounded to two decimal places.
- Returns 0.00 if there are no shift workers in the company.
Pre-condition:
- The company instance exists.
- The company's employees list is initialized; it may be empty.
- Each ShiftWorker belongs to the Delivery department, and its setdepartment(department : String) method must have been called with a non-null department string equal to "Delivery".
Post-condition:
- No persistent state is modified.
- The returned value equals the sum of all shift workers' holiday premiums, rounded to two decimal places.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title employee Model

class Company {
    name : String
    departments : List<Department>
    employees : List<Employee>
    calculateTotalEmployeeSalary() : double
    calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission() : double
    calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums() : double
}

    type : DepartmentType
    company : Company

    manager : Manager
    calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours() : double
}
abstract class Employee {
    birthDate : EDate
    department : String
    name : String
    socialInsuranceNumber : String
    company : Company
}
class Manager {
    position : String
    salary : double
    subordinates : List<Employee>
    getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount() : int
}
class OffShiftWorker {
    officialHolidayPermit : boolean
    weekendPermit : boolean
}
class SalesPeople {
    amountOfSales : double
    commissionPercentage : double
    salary : double
    getTotalCommission() : double
}
class ShiftWorker {
    holidayPremium : double
    calculateHolidayPremium() : double
    setdepartment(department : String) : void
}
abstract class Worker {
    hourlyRates : double
    weeklyWorkingHour : int
}
enum DepartmentType {
    PRODUCTION
    CONTROL
    DELIVERY
}

Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Company *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department --> "*" Employee : employees
Department --> Manager : manager
Employee --> Company : company  'opposite: employees
Manager --|> Employee
Manager --> "*" Employee : subordinates

SalesPeople --|> Employee
ShiftWorker --|> Worker
Worker --|> Employee

- No persistent state is modified.
- The returned value equals the shift worker's holiday premium.

# Operation Specifications
Department::calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours
Summary:
Calculate the average weekly working hours of all Worker employees in this department.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize sumHours to 0 and workerCount to 0.
2. Iterate over the department's employees list.
3. For each employee that is a Worker instance:
   3.1 Add weeklyWorkingHour to sumHours.
   3.2 Increment workerCount by 1.
4. If workerCount is 0, return 0.
5. Compute average = sumHours / workerCount.
6. Round average to two decimal places.
7. Return average.
Related Features:
- employees : List<Employee>
- calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours() : double
- weeklyWorkingHour : int
Related Classes: Employee, Worker
Input:
- None.
- Operates on the current Department instance.
Output:
- double value representing the average weekly working hours of workers in the department.
- The value is rounded to two decimal places.
- Returns 0 when the department has no workers.
Pre-condition:
- The department instance exists.
- The department's employees list is initialized; it may be empty.
Post-condition:
- No persistent state is modified.
- The returned value equals the average of all workers' weekly working hours, rounded to two decimal places, or 0 if there are no workers.

   * Summary:
   * Calculate the average weekly working hours of all Worker employees in this department.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Initialize sumHours to 0 and workerCount to 0.
   * 2. Iterate over the department's employees list.
   * 3. For each employee that is a Worker instance:
   *    3.1 Add weeklyWorkingHour to sumHours.
   *    3.2 Increment workerCount by 1.
   * 4. If workerCount is 0, return 0.
   * 5. Compute average = sumHours / workerCount.
   * 6. Round average to two decimal places.
   * 7. Return average.
   * Related Features:
   * - employees : List<Employee>
   * - calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours() : double
   * - weeklyWorkingHour : int
   * Related Classes: Employee, Worker
   * Input:
   * - None.
   * - Operates on the current Department instance.
   * Output:
   * - double value representing the average weekly working hours of workers in the department.
   * - The value is rounded to two decimal places.
   * - Returns 0 when the department has no workers.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - The department instance exists.
   * - The department's employees list is initialized; it may be empty.
   * Post-condition:
   * - No persistent state is modified.
   * - The returned value equals the average of all workers' weekly working hours, rounded to two decimal places, or 0 if there are no workers.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours()
  {
    int sumHours = 0;
    int workerCount = 0;

    for (Employee employee : getEmployees())
    {
      if (employee instanceof Worker)
      {
        sumHours += ((Worker)employee).getWeeklyWorkingHour();
        workerCount++;
      }
    }

    if (workerCount == 0)
    {
      return 0;
    }

    double average = (double)sumHours / workerCount;
    return Math.round(average * 100.0) / 100.0;
    
  }

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

   * 1. Initialize totalSalary to 0.
   * 2. For each employee in the company's employees list:
   *    2.1 If the employee is a Worker, calculate weeklyWorkingHour * hourlyRates.
   *    2.2 If the worker is a ShiftWorker, add the holiday premium.

  public double calculateTotalEmployeeSalary()
  {
    double totalSalary = 0.0;
    for (Employee employee : getEmployees())
    {
      double salary = 0.0;
      if (employee instanceof Worker)
      {
        Worker worker = (Worker)employee;
        salary = worker.getWeeklyWorkingHour() * worker.getHourlyRates();
        if (employee instanceof ShiftWorker)
        {
          salary += ((ShiftWorker)employee).getHolidayPremium();
        }
      }
      else if (employee instanceof SalesPeople)
      {
        SalesPeople salesPeople = (SalesPeople)employee;
        salary = salesPeople.getSalary() + salesPeople.getAmountOfSales() * salesPeople.getCommissionPercentage();
      }
      else if (employee instanceof Manager)
      {
        salary = ((Manager)employee).getSalary();
      }
      totalSalary += salary;
    }
    return Math.round(totalSalary * 100.0) / 100.0;
    
  }

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title employee Model

class Company {
    name : String
    departments : List<Department>
    employees : List<Employee>
    calculateTotalEmployeeSalary() : double
    calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission() : double
    calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums() : double
}
class Department {
    type : DepartmentType
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    manager : Manager
    calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours() : double
}
abstract class Employee {
    birthDate : EDate
    department : String
    name : String
    socialInsuranceNumber : String
    company : Company
}
class Manager {
    position : String
    salary : double
    subordinates : List<Employee>
    getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount() : int
}
class OffShiftWorker {
    officialHolidayPermit : boolean
    weekendPermit : boolean
}
class SalesPeople {
    amountOfSales : double
    commissionPercentage : double
    salary : double
    getTotalCommission() : double
}
class ShiftWorker {
    holidayPremium : double
    calculateHolidayPremium() : double
    setdepartment(department : String) : void
}
abstract class Worker {
    hourlyRates : double
    weeklyWorkingHour : int
}
enum DepartmentType {
    PRODUCTION
    CONTROL
    DELIVERY
}

Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Company *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department --> "*" Employee : employees
Department --> Manager : manager
Employee --> Company : company  'opposite: employees
Manager --|> Employee
Manager --> "*" Employee : subordinates
OffShiftWorker --|> Worker
SalesPeople --|> Employee
ShiftWorker --|> Worker
Worker --|> Employee
@enduml
```

5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

    weekendPermit : boolean

    CONTROL
    DELIVERY
}

    officialHolidayPermit : boolean
    weekendPermit : boolean
}

    commissionPercentage : double
    salary : double
    getTotalCommission() : double
}
class ShiftWorker {
    holidayPremium : double
    calculateHolidayPremium() : double
    setdepartment(department : String) : void
}

    hourlyRates : double
    weeklyWorkingHour : int
}

    PRODUCTION
    CONTROL
    DELIVERY
}

# Operation Specifications
Manager::getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount
Summary:
Return the number of direct subordinate employees managed by this manager.
Algorithm:
1. Read the manager's subordinates list.
2. Return the size of the list.
Related Features:
- Manager.subordinates
- Manager.position
- Employee.name
Related Classes: Employee, Manager
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Manager instance and its subordinates collection.
Output:
- Returns an int representing the number of direct subordinate employees.
- Returns 0 if the manager has no direct subordinates.
Pre-condition:
- The Manager instance must exist.
- The subordinates list may be empty but must not be null.
Post-condition:
- The manager state is not modified.
- The returned value equals the number of elements in the subordinates list.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Operation Specifications
Department::calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours
Summary:
Calculate the average weekly working hours of all Worker employees in this department.
Only Worker instances are considered. If the department contains no workers, return 0.
The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize workerCount = 0 and totalHours = 0.
2. Iterate through this department's employees list.
3. For each employee, if it is a Worker instance:
   - Add weeklyWorkingHour to totalHours.
   - Increment workerCount.
4. If workerCount == 0, return 0.
5. Compute average = totalHours / workerCount.
6. Round average to two decimal places.
7. Return average.
Related Features:
- Department.employees
- Department.type
- Worker.weeklyWorkingHour
Related Classes: Department, Employee, Worker, OffShiftWorker, ShiftWorker
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Department instance and its employees collection.
- weeklyWorkingHour is expected to be a non-negative integer.
Output:
- Returns a double representing the average weekly working hours of all workers in the department.
- The value is rounded to two decimal places.
- Returns 0.00 when the department has no Worker employees.
Pre-condition:
- The Department instance must exist.
- The employees list may be empty but must not be null.
- Worker-related attributes must be initialized for all Worker instances.
Post-condition:
- The department state is not modified.
- The returned value equals the average weeklyWorkingHour of all Worker instances in the department.
- The returned value is rounded to two decimal places.

 */

/*
 * revised description for this operation_2

Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Operation Specifications
Company::calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums
Summary:
Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all ShiftWorker employees in the company.
The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize totalPremium = 0.
2. Iterate through the company's employees list.
3. For each employee, if it is a ShiftWorker instance:
   - Add calculateHolidayPremium() to totalPremium.
4. Round totalPremium to two decimal places.
5. Return totalPremium.
Related Features:
- Company.employees
- ShiftWorker.holidayPremium
- ShiftWorker.calculateHolidayPremium()
- ShiftWorker.setdepartment(department : String)
Related Classes: Employee, ShiftWorker, Department
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Company instance and its employees collection.
- ShiftWorker employees must belong only to the Delivery department.
Output:
- Returns a double representing the sum of holiday premiums for all ShiftWorkers.
- The value is rounded to two decimal places.
- Returns 0.00 if there are no ShiftWorkers in the company.
Pre-condition:
- The Company instance must exist.
- The employees list may be empty but must not be null.
- Every ShiftWorker in the company must already have a valid Delivery department assignment.
Post-condition:
- The company state is not modified.
- The returned value equals the sum of holiday premiums of all ShiftWorkers.
- The returned value is rounded to two decimal places.

Company::calculateTotalEmployeeSalary
Summary:
Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company by summing:
- each Worker salary = weeklyWorkingHour * hourlyRates, plus holiday premium for ShiftWorker;
- each SalesPeople salary = salary + amountOfSales * commissionPercentage;
- each Manager salary = salary.
The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize totalSalary = 0.
2. Iterate through the company's employees list.
3. For each employee:
   - If the employee is a ShiftWorker, compute weeklyWorkingHour * hourlyRates + calculateHolidayPremium().
   - Else if the employee is a Worker (including OffShiftWorker), compute weeklyWorkingHour * hourlyRates.
   - Else if the employee is a SalesPeople, compute salary + getTotalCommission().
   - Else if the employee is a Manager, compute salary.
4. Add the computed value to totalSalary.
5. Round totalSalary to two decimal places.
6. Return totalSalary.
Related Features:
- Company.employees
- Company.name
- Employee.company
- Worker.hourlyRates
- Worker.weeklyWorkingHour
- SalesPeople.salary
- SalesPeople.amountOfSales
- SalesPeople.commissionPercentage
- Manager.salary
- ShiftWorker.holidayPremium
- SalesPeople.getTotalCommission()
- ShiftWorker.calculateHolidayPremium()
Related Classes: Employee, Worker, SalesPeople, Manager, ShiftWorker, OffShiftWorker
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Company instance and its employees collection.
- Employee values used in the calculation must be non-negative for salary-related fields and working hours as per business data integrity.
Output:
- Returns a double representing the total salary of all employees in the company.
- The value is rounded to two decimal places.
- If the company has no employees, returns 0.00.
Pre-condition:
- The Company instance must exist.
- The employees list may be empty but must not be null.
- Each employee referenced by the company should have valid type-specific attributes initialized.
- For ShiftWorker employees, their department must already satisfy the Delivery-only rule.
Post-condition:
- The company state is not modified.
- The returned value equals the sum of all employee salaries computed according to their type.
- The returned value is rounded to two decimal places.

Company::calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission
Summary:
Calculate the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company.
The total commission equals the sum of amountOfSales * commissionPercentage for each SalesPeople.
The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize totalCommission = 0.
2. Iterate through the company's employees list.
3. For each employee, if it is a SalesPeople instance:
   - Compute amountOfSales * commissionPercentage.
   - Add the result to totalCommission.
4. Round totalCommission to two decimal places.
5. Return totalCommission.
Related Features:
- Company.employees
- SalesPeople.amountOfSales
- SalesPeople.commissionPercentage
- SalesPeople.getTotalCommission()
Related Classes: Employee, SalesPeople
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Company instance and its employees collection.
- commissionPercentage is expected to be expressed as a fractional rate (for example, 0.05 for 5%).
Output:
- Returns a double representing the sum of all salespeople commissions.
- The value is rounded to two decimal places.
- Returns 0.00 if there are no salespeople in the company.
Pre-condition:
- The Company instance must exist.
- The employees list may be empty but must not be null.
- SalesPeople fields amountOfSales and commissionPercentage must be initialized.
Post-condition:
- The company state is not modified.
- The returned value equals the sum of each SalesPeople's commission.
- The returned value is rounded to two decimal places.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

- holidayPremium must be initialized.
- The shift worker's department must be Delivery, enforced by setdepartment().

- The shift worker state is not modified.

Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

    weekendPermit : boolean

Employee --> Company : company  'opposite: employees

# Operation Specifications
ShiftWorker::calculateHolidayPremium
Summary:
Calculate the holiday premium amount for this shift worker.
The value is based on the stored holidayPremium attribute.
Algorithm:
1. Read holidayPremium.
2. Return holidayPremium.
Related Features:
- ShiftWorker.holidayPremium
- ShiftWorker.setdepartment(department : String)
- Worker.hourlyRates
- Worker.weeklyWorkingHour
Related Classes: ShiftWorker
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current ShiftWorker instance.
Output:
- Returns a double representing the holiday premium amount.
- The value may be 0 if no premium is configured.
Pre-condition:
- The ShiftWorker instance must exist.
- holidayPremium must be initialized.
- The shift worker's department must be Delivery, enforced by setdepartment().
Post-condition:
- The shift worker state is not modified.
- The returned value equals the stored holidayPremium.

  1.3 Generate the class summarization.

4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Calculate the average weekly working hours of all Worker employees in this department.
   * Only Worker instances are considered. If the department contains no workers, return 0.
   * The result is rounded to two decimal places.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Initialize workerCount = 0 and totalHours = 0.
   * 2. Iterate through this department's employees list.
   * 3. For each employee, if it is a Worker instance:
   *    - Add weeklyWorkingHour to totalHours.
   *    - Increment workerCount.
   * 4. If workerCount == 0, return 0.
   * 5. Compute average = totalHours / workerCount.
   * 6. Round average to two decimal places.
   * 7. Return average.
   * Related Features:
   * - Department.employees
   * - Department.type
   * - Worker.weeklyWorkingHour
   * Related Classes: Department, Employee, Worker, OffShiftWorker, ShiftWorker
   * Input:
   * - No explicit input parameters.
   * - Uses the current Department instance and its employees collection.
   * - weeklyWorkingHour is expected to be a non-negative integer.
   * Output:
   * - Returns a double representing the average weekly working hours of all workers in the department.
   * - The value is rounded to two decimal places.
   * - Returns 0.00 when the department has no Worker employees.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - The Department instance must exist.
   * - The employees list may be empty but must not be null.
   * - Worker-related attributes must be initialized for all Worker instances.
   * Post-condition:

   * - The returned value equals the average weeklyWorkingHour of all Worker instances in the department.

   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours()
  {
    int workerCount = 0;
    double totalHours = 0.0;

    for (Employee employee : getEmployees())
    {
      if (employee instanceof Worker)
      {
        totalHours += ((Worker)employee).getWeeklyWorkingHour();
        workerCount++;
      }
    }

    if (workerCount == 0)
    {
      return 0.0;
    }

    double average = totalHours / workerCount;
    return Math.round(average * 100.0) / 100.0;
    
  }

   *    - Else if the employee is a SalesPeople, compute salary + getTotalCommission().
   *    - Else if the employee is a Manager, compute salary.
   * 4. Add the computed value to totalSalary.
   * 5. Round totalSalary to two decimal places.
   * 6. Return totalSalary.
   * Related Features:

   * Summary:
   * Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company by summing:
   * - each Worker salary = weeklyWorkingHour * hourlyRates, plus holiday premium for ShiftWorker;
   * - each SalesPeople salary = salary + amountOfSales * commissionPercentage;
   * - each Manager salary = salary.
   * The result is rounded to two decimal places.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Initialize totalSalary = 0.
   * 2. Iterate through the company's employees list.
   * 3. For each employee:
   *    - If the employee is a ShiftWorker, compute weeklyWorkingHour * hourlyRates + calculateHolidayPremium().
   *    - Else if the employee is a Worker (including OffShiftWorker), compute weeklyWorkingHour * hourlyRates.
   *    - Else if the employee is a SalesPeople, compute salary + getTotalCommission().
   *    - Else if the employee is a Manager, compute salary.
   * 4. Add the computed value to totalSalary.
   * 5. Round totalSalary to two decimal places.
   * 6. Return totalSalary.

  public double calculateTotalEmployeeSalary()
  {
    double totalSalary = 0.0;
    for (Employee employee : getEmployees())
    {
      if (employee instanceof ShiftWorker)
      {
        ShiftWorker shiftWorker = (ShiftWorker)employee;
        totalSalary += shiftWorker.getWeeklyWorkingHour() * shiftWorker.getHourlyRates() + shiftWorker.calculateHolidayPremium();
      }
      else if (employee instanceof Worker)
      {
        Worker worker = (Worker)employee;
        totalSalary += worker.getWeeklyWorkingHour() * worker.getHourlyRates();
      }
      else if (employee instanceof SalesPeople)
      {
        SalesPeople salesPeople = (SalesPeople)employee;
        totalSalary += salesPeople.getSalary() + salesPeople.getTotalCommission();
      }
      else if (employee instanceof Manager)
      {
        Manager manager = (Manager)employee;
        totalSalary += manager.getSalary();
      }
    }
    return Math.round(totalSalary * 100.0) / 100.0;
    
  }
